[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
The remnants of a beautifully wrought iron chandelier hang from the roof, just above a decayed wooden table. The room is smaller than the main dining room, perhaps the personal dining area of the master of the tower. The tapestries that still hang on the walls reveal in faded color that they were once masterpieces. A sodden piece of cloth covers the floor beneath the rotten wood table, it appears to be the hide of some great furred beast, but the water has damaged it beyond recognition. Because of the awkward tilting of the tower, much of the water streaming through the rest of the first floor has made its way here. Years of corrosive water has finally worn away the thick stone wall and the water has at last found a way out of the tower via dozens of holes where large stones once were. The water cascades out the holes in a constant stream into a cavern so dark no amount of light seems able to penetrate it.

The area is illuminated.

The enclosed entrance opens up into a wide, rounded room. The walls are made of large stone blocks stacked precariously upon one another and determined plant life has forced its way through the mortar between the rocks and now grows unchecked across the walls and toward the towering ceiling. The floor here is steeply angled down toward the east, not surprising considering that the tower's foundation has begun to crumble from age and neglect.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Harru the Galirrim,  Waysai the Galirrim,  Vandit the Galirrim, 1st living mirage, 1st bog water elemental
Obvious exits are: south, north

The floor here is steeply angled down toward the east, not surprising considering that the tower's foundation has begun to crumble from age and neglect.

The tower is far more dilapidated on the inside than the outside first suggested. The scent of molding wood hangs heavy and oppressive in the motionless air. [Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
The remnants of a beautifully wrought iron chandelier hang from the roof, just above a decayed wooden table. The room is smaller than the main dining room, perhaps the personal dining area of the master of the tower. The tapestries that still hang on the walls reveal in faded color that they were once masterpieces. A sodden piece of cloth covers the floor beneath the rotten wood table, it appears to be the hide of some great furred beast, but the water has damaged it beyond recognition. Because of the awkward tilting of the tower, much of the water streaming through the rest of the first floor has made its way here. Years of corrosive water has finally worn away the thick stone wall and the water has at last found a way out of the tower via dozens of holes where large stones once were. The water cascades out the holes in a constant stream into a cavern so dark no amount of light seems able to penetrate it.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Harru the Galirrim,  Waysai the Galirrim,  Vandit the Galirrim
Obvious exits are: north

[Dremsyl's Tower, Kitchen]

[Dremsyl's Tower, Storage Pantry]

[Dremsyl's Tower, First Floor Hallway]
Broken wooden beams have fallen from the ceiling, and landed in the center of this room, somewhat blocking the flow of water from the west. Mud has piled against the beams, creating a natural dam and a perfect home of all sorts of small creatures. Even a frog or two has made its way down here and grown fat off the abundant insects living in the muddy mess.

Piles of useless furniture line the walls, the wood decayed and the metal rusted beyond use. The ceiling above is cracked and some of the rock has fallen, allowing glimpses into the above floor and revealing only more darkness. Strange fleshy tentacles have pushed through the small gaps in the ceiling and now creep along the stone, ending only a few feet short of the walls.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, east

[Dremsyl's Tower, First Floor Entryway]
A few inches of cold water ripples across the floor and plant roots run down the walls and into the dirty water, making footing treacherous. [Dremsyl's Tower, First Floor Stairwell]
The incessant sound of dripping water echoes through the small room as small rivulets of brackish water force their way through tiny cracks in the stone walls, and slowly trickle down to the sodden floor. Rust has run down the walls from the ceiling above and an unidentifiable odor wafts through the area, overpowering even the mildew. A spiraling set of stone steps leads further into the heights of the tower, daring any adventurer who has survived this long, to venture further into the perverted tower.

The area is illuminated.

[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
Wooden tables running the length of the room have become swollen with moisture from the thin stream of water running across the floor to the kitchens. The dark blur of an enslaved goblin darts across the room occasionally, a strange white hat on its head, gathering mushrooms and fungus from moldering tapestries which still desperately cling to the stone walls. [Dremsyl's Tower, Kitchen]
A massive stone oven rests against the far wall, a fire burns hotly below it as a goblin stands before the flames with a long stick, roasting various mushrooms, frogs, and insects. [Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
The remnants of a beautifully wrought iron chandelier hang from the roof, just above a decayed wooden table. The room is smaller than the main dining room, perhaps the personal dining area of the master of the tower. The tapestries that still hang on the walls reveal in faded color that they were once masterpieces. A sodden piece of cloth covers the floor beneath the rotten wood table, it appears to be the hide of some great furred beast, but the water has damaged it beyond recognition. Because of the awkward tilting of the tower, much of the water streaming through the rest of the first floor has made its way here. Years of corrosive water has finally worn away the thick stone wall and the water has at last found a way out of the tower via dozens of holes where large stones once were. The water cascades out the holes in a constant stream into a cavern so dark no amount of light seems able to penetrate it.

The area is illuminated.

[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
The remnants of a beautifully wrought iron chandelier hang from the roof, just above a decayed wooden table. The room is smaller than the main dining room, perhaps the personal dining area of the master of the tower. The tapestries that still hang on the walls reveal in faded color that they were once masterpieces. A sodden piece of cloth covers the floor beneath the rotten wood table, it appears to be the hide of some great furred beast, but the water has damaged it beyond recognition. Because of the awkward tilting of the tower, much of the water streaming through the rest of the first floor has made its way here. Years of corrosive water has finally worn away the thick stone wall and the water has at last found a way out of the tower via dozens of holes where large stones once were. The water cascades out the holes in a constant stream into a cavern so dark no amount of light seems able to penetrate it.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Harru the Galirrim,  Waysai the Galirrim,  Vandit the Galirrim, 1st malformed fungus goblin

A massive stone oven rests against the far wall, a fire burns hotly below it as a goblin stands before the flames with a long stick, roasting various mushrooms, frogs, and insects. The goblin seems more disheveled than the average goblin, its hair is wild, grayed, and falling out in clumps and its skin is pallid, suggesting that the poor creature has never seen the sun before. It seems intent on its dinner, and is oblivious to everything else around it.

[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
Wooden tables running the length of the room have become swollen with moisture from the thin stream of water running across the floor to the kitchens. Benches lay overturned on the ground, and cracked clay dishes have been forgotten on the tabletops. The dark blur of an enslaved goblin darts across the room occasionally, a strange white hat on its head, gathering mushrooms and fungus from moldering tapestries which still desperately cling to the stone walls. The occasional angry croak or chirp, suggests that fungus isn't the only thing the goblin is interested in.

[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
The remnants of a beautifully wrought iron chandelier hang from the roof, just above a decayed wooden table. The room is smaller than the main dining room, perhaps the personal dining area of the master of the tower. The tapestries that still hang on the walls reveal in faded color that they were once masterpieces. A sodden piece of cloth covers the floor beneath the rotten wood table, it appears to be the hide of some great furred beast, but the water has damaged it beyond recognition. Because of the awkward tilting of the tower, much of the water streaming through the rest of the first floor has made its way here. Years of corrosive water has finally worn away the thick stone wall and the water has at last found a way out of the tower via dozens of holes where large stones once were. The water cascades out the holes in a constant stream into a cavern so dark no amount of light seems able to penetrate it.

In the center of the space is a large pile of grimy blankets, the scent of unwashed bodies emanates from the pile, suggesting that this is the sleeping place of the goblins and has been for a very long time. Young goblins scamper around the pile of filthy fabrics, obviously the unlivable condition of the tower hasn't stopped the goblins from rampant breeding.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Harru the Galirrim,  Waysai the Galirrim,  Vandit the Galirrim
Obvious exits are: south, north

[Dremsyl's Tower, Storage Pantry]

[Dremsyl's Tower, Kitchen]
A massive stone oven rests against the far wall, a fire burns hotly below it as a goblin stands before the flames with a long stick, roasting various mushrooms, frogs, and insects. The goblin seems more disheveled than the average goblin, its hair is wild, grayed, and falling out in clumps and its skin is pallid, suggesting that the poor creature has never seen the sun before. It seems intent on its dinner, and is oblivious to everything else around it.

[Dremsyl's Tower, Dining Room]
Wooden tables running the length of the room have become swollen with moisture from the thin stream of water running across the floor to the kitchens. Benches lay overturned on the ground, and cracked clay dishes have been forgotten on the tabletops. The dark blur of an enslaved goblin darts across the room occasionally, a strange white hat on its head, gathering mushrooms and fungus from moldering tapestries which still desperately cling to the stone walls. The occasional angry croak or chirp, suggests that fungus isn't the only thing the goblin is interested in.

The area is illuminated.

[Merchant Square]
Smoke steadily rises from the chimneys of this massive stone building on the north side of the well maintained square. Through the open facade, young Vorngar can be seen pumping the bellows while the master heats a rod of glowing iron within the forge.  The clanking sound of metal on metal can be heard as the gifted apprentices create and sharpen weapons of all types. The hot fires of the forge radiate all around. A large sign mounted above a tall studded iron door clearly reads, "Talafon's Weapons and Armor".  On the south side of the road a small colorful building reflects light cast from two glowing orbs mounted on each side of a glittering portal floating between a silver archway. Above the archway a small, but functional sign reads, "Malinar's Magic and Mayhem".

The area is illuminated.

[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

The neat brick storefront of shops peak out around the perimeter of the cobblestone square along with neatly manicured hedges with trees spaced between them. Detailed arches, sculpted in the memory of iconic leaders that fought for Urda's freedom, decorate the busy square. The Eastern Cross leads travelers to the east gate and towards the Bloodlands. A freshly painted clapboard building, styled like a ship, faces southwards towards the street.

The area is illuminated.

[Merchant Square]
Smoke steadily rises from the chimneys of this massive stone building on the north side of the well maintained square. Through the open facade, young Vorngar can be seen pumping the bellows while the master heats a rod of glowing iron within the forge.  The clanking sound of metal on metal can be heard as the gifted apprentices create and sharpen weapons of all types. The hot fires of the forge radiate all around. A large sign mounted above a tall studded iron door clearly reads, "Talafon's Weapons and Armor".  On the south side of the road a small colorful building reflects light cast from two glowing orbs mounted on each side of a glittering portal floating between a silver archway. Above the archway a small, but functional sign reads, "Malinar's Magic and Mayhem".

The area is illuminated.
